```
1→"""Aggregate mechanical detector findings into structured evidence clusters.
     2→
     3→Reads ALL state findings and produces signal clusters organized for holistic
     4→review context enrichment.  The LLM reviewer gets hotspots and patterns —
     5→not raw finding dumps — so it can investigate root causes.
     6→"""
     7→
     8→from __future__ import annotations
     9→
    10→from collections import Counter, defaultdict
    11→from typing import Any
    12→
    13→
    14→def _normalize_allowed_files(
    15→    allowed_files: set[str] | list[str] | tuple[str, ...] | None,
    16→) -> set[str] | None:
    17→    """Normalize optional allowed-file scope to slash-normalized relative paths."""
    18→    if allowed_files is None:
    19→        return None
    20→    out: set[str] = set()
    21→    for raw in allowed_files:
    22→        if not isinstance(raw, str):
    23→            continue
    24→        file_path = raw.strip().replace("\\", "/")
    25→        if file_path:
    26→            out.add(file_path)
    27→    return out
    28→
    29→
    30→def _normalized_finding_file(finding: dict[str, Any]) -> str:
    31→    filepath = finding.get("file", "")
    32→    if not isinstance(filepath, str):
    33→        return ""
    34→    return filepath.strip().replace("\\", "/")
    35→
    36→
    37→def _bucket_open_findings(
    38→    findings: dict[str, Any],
    39→    *,
    40→    allowed_scope: set[str] | None,
    41→) -> tuple[
    42→    dict[str, list[dict]],
    43→    dict[str, list[dict]],
    44→    Counter[str],
    45→    dict[str, list[str]],
    46→]:
    47→    by_detector: dict[str, list[dict]] = defaultdict(list)
    48→    by_file: dict[str, list[dict]] = defaultdict(list)
    49→    smell_counter: Counter[str] = Counter()
    50→    smell_files: dict[str, list[str]] = defaultdict(list)
    51→
    52→    for finding in findings.values():
    53→        if not isinstance(finding, dict) or finding.get("status") != "open":
    54→            continue
    55→        normalized_file = _normalized_finding_file(finding)
    56→        if allowed_scope is not None and normalized_file not in allowed_scope:
    57→            continue
    58→
    59→        detector = finding.get("detector", "")
    60→        if detector:
    61→            by_detector[detector].append(finding)
    62→        if normalized_file and normalized_file != ".":
    63→            by_file[normalized_file].append(finding)
    64→
    65→        if detector != "smells":
    66→            continue
    67→        detail = finding.get("detail", {})
    68→        smell_id = detail.get("smell_id", "") if isinstance(detail, dict) else ""
    69→        if smell_id:
    70→            smell_counter[smell_id] += 1
    71→            smell_files[smell_id].append(normalized_file)
    72→
    73→    return by_detector, by_file, smell_counter, smell_files
    74→
    75→
    76→def _attach_cluster(evidence: dict[str, Any], key: str, value: Any) -> None:
    77→    if value:
    78→        evidence[key] = value
    79→
    80→
    81→def _build_mechanical_clusters(
    82→    by_detector: dict[str, list[dict]],
    83→    by_file: dict[str, list[dict]],
    84→    smell_counter: Counter[str],
    85→    smell_files: dict[str, list[str]],
    86→) -> dict[str, Any]:
    87→    evidence: dict[str, Any] = {}
    88→    _attach_cluster(
    89→        evidence,
    90→        "complexity_hotspots",
    91→        _build_complexity_hotspots(by_detector, by_file),
    92→    )
    93→    _attach_cluster(evidence, "error_hotspots", _build_error_hotspots(by_detector))
    94→    _attach_cluster(evidence, "mutable_globals", _build_mutable_globals(by_detector))
    95→    _attach_cluster(
    96→        evidence, "boundary_violations", _build_boundary_violations(by_detector)
    97→    )
    98→    _attach_cluster(evidence, "dead_code", _build_dead_code(by_detector))
    99→    _attach_cluster(evidence, "private_crossings", _build_private_crossings(by_detector))
   100→    _attach_cluster(
   101→        evidence, "deferred_import_density", _build_deferred_import_density(by_file)
   102→    )
   103→    _attach_cluster(
   104→        evidence, "duplicate_clusters", _build_duplicate_clusters(by_detector)
   105→    )
   106→    _attach_cluster(evidence, "naming_drift", _build_naming_drift(by_detector))
   107→    _attach_cluster(evidence, "flat_dir_findings", _build_flat_dir_findings(by_detector))
   108→    _attach_cluster(
   109→        evidence, "large_file_distribution", _build_large_file_distribution(by_detector)
   110→    )
   111→    _attach_cluster(evidence, "security_hotspots", _build_security_hotspots(by_detector))
   112→    _attach_cluster(evidence, "signal_density", _build_signal_density(by_file))
   113→    _attach_cluster(
   114→        evidence, "systemic_patterns", _build_systemic_patterns(smell_counter, smell_files)
   115→    )
   116→    return evidence
   117→
   118→
   119→def gather_mechanical_evidence(
   120→    state: dict[str, Any],
   121→    *,
   122→    allowed_files: set[str] | list[str] | tuple[str, ...] | None = None,
   123→) -> dict[str, Any]:
   124→    """Aggregate open findings into evidence clusters for holistic review.
   125→
   126→    Returns a dict of named clusters.  Empty dict when no findings exist.
   127→    """
   128→    findings = state.get("findings", {})
   129→    if not findings:
   130→        return {}
   131→    allowed_scope = _normalize_allowed_files(allowed_files)
   132→
   133→    by_detector, by_file, smell_counter, smell_files = _bucket_open_findings(
   134→        findings, allowed_scope=allowed_scope
   135→    )
   136→    if not by_detector:
   137→        return {}
   138→    return _build_mechanical_clusters(by_detector, by_file, smell_counter, smell_files)
   139→
   140→
   141→# ── Cluster builders ─────────────────────────────────────────────────
   142→
   143→
   144→def _get_detail(finding: dict, key: str, default: Any = None) -> Any:
   145→    detail = finding.get("detail", {})
   146→    if not isinstance(detail, dict):
   147→        return default
   148→    return detail.get(key, default)
   149→
   150→
   151→def _detail_payload(finding: dict) -> dict[str, Any]:
   152→    detail = finding.get("detail", {})
   153→    payload = dict(detail) if isinstance(detail, dict) else {}
   154→    payload.setdefault("name", "")
   155→    payload.setdefault("function", "")
   156→    payload.setdefault("files", [])
   157→    payload.setdefault("kind", "")
   158→    payload.setdefault("reason", "")
   159→    payload.setdefault("symbol", "")
   160→    payload.setdefault("source", "")
   161→    payload.setdefault("target", "")
   162→    payload.setdefault("imported_from", "")
   163→    payload.setdefault("direction", "")
   164→    payload.setdefault("violation", "")
   165→    payload.setdefault("expected_convention", "")
   166→    payload.setdefault("file_count", 0)
   167→    payload.setdefault("score", payload.get("combined_score", 0))
   168→    payload.setdefault("combined_score", payload.get("score", 0))
   169→    return payload
   170→
   171→
   172→def _get_signals(finding: dict) -> dict:
   173→    detail = finding.get("detail", {})
   174→    if not isinstance(detail, dict):
   175→        return {}
   176→    signals = detail.get("signals", {})
   177→    return signals if isinstance(signals, dict) else {}
   178→
   179→
   180→def _safe_num(value: Any, default: float = 0.0) -> float:
   181→    if isinstance(value, bool):
   182→        return default
   183→    if isinstance(value, int | float):
   184→        return float(value)
   185→    return default
   186→
   187→
   188→def _empty_complexity_entry(filepath: str) -> dict[str, Any]:
   189→    return {
   190→        "file": filepath,
   191→        "loc": 0,
   192→        "complexity_score": 0,
   193→        "signals": [],
   194→        "component_count": 0,
   195→        "function_count": 0,
   196→        "monster_functions": 0,
   197→        "cyclomatic_hotspots": 0,
   198→    }
   199→
   200→
   201→def _update_complexity_entry(entry: dict[str, Any], signals: dict) -> None:
   202→    entry["loc"] = max(entry["loc"], _safe_num(signals.get("loc")))
   203→    entry["function_count"] = max(
   204→        entry["function_count"], _safe_num(signals.get("function_count"))
   205→    )
   206→    entry["component_count"] = max(
   207→        entry["component_count"], _safe_num(signals.get("component_count"))
   208→    )
   209→    max_params = _safe_num(signals.get("max_params"))
   210→    if max_params >= 5:
   211→        entry["signals"].append(f"{int(max_params)} params")
   212→    max_nesting = _safe_num(signals.get("max_nesting"))
   213→    if max_nesting >= 4:
   214→        entry["signals"].append(f"nesting depth {int(max_nesting)}")
   215→    complexity = _safe_num(signals.get("complexity_score"))
   216→    entry["complexity_score"] = max(entry["complexity_score"], complexity)
   217→
   218→
   219→def _apply_smell_complexity_counts(
   220→    file_data: dict[str, dict[str, Any]],
   221→    by_detector: dict[str, list[dict]],
   222→) -> None:
   223→    for finding in by_detector.get("smells", []):
   224→        filepath = finding.get("file", "")
   225→        if filepath not in file_data:
   226→            continue
   227→        smell_id = _get_detail(finding, "smell_id", "")
   228→        if smell_id == "monster_function":
   229→            file_data[filepath]["monster_functions"] += 1
   230→        elif smell_id in ("cyclomatic_complexity", "high_cyclomatic"):
   231→            file_data[filepath]["cyclomatic_hotspots"] += 1
   232→
   233→
   234→def _apply_cohesion_complexity_counts(
   235→    file_data: dict[str, dict[str, Any]],
   236→    by_detector: dict[str, list[dict]],
   237→) -> None:
   238→    for finding in by_detector.get("responsibility_cohesion", []):
   239→        filepath = finding.get("file", "")
   240→        if filepath not in file_data:
   241→            continue
   242→        clusters = _safe_num(_get_detail(finding, "cluster_count"))
   243→        file_data[filepath]["component_count"] = max(
   244→            file_data[filepath]["component_count"], clusters
   245→        )
   246→
   247→
   248→def _rank_complexity_entries(file_data: dict[str, dict[str, Any]]) -> list[dict]:
   249→    for entry in file_data.values():
   250→        entry["_score"] = (
   251→            entry["loc"] / 100
   252→            + entry["complexity_score"]
   253→            + entry["component_count"] * 3
   254→            + entry["monster_functions"] * 10
   255→        )
   256→        entry["signals"] = list(dict.fromkeys(entry["signals"]))
   257→    ranked = sorted(file_data.values(), key=lambda e: -e["_score"])[:20]
   258→    for entry in ranked:
   259→        del entry["_score"]
   260→    return ranked
   261→
   262→
   263→def _build_complexity_hotspots(
   264→    by_detector: dict[str, list[dict]],
   265→    by_file: dict[str, list[dict]],
   266→) -> list[dict]:
   267→    """Top 20 files by composite complexity score."""
   268→    file_data: dict[str, dict[str, Any]] = {}
   269→
   270→    for finding in by_detector.get("structural", []):
   271→        filepath = finding.get("file", "")
   272→        if not filepath:
   273→            continue
   274→        signals = _get_signals(finding)
   275→        entry = file_data.setdefault(filepath, _empty_complexity_entry(filepath))
   276→        _update_complexity_entry(entry, signals)
   277→
   278→    _apply_smell_complexity_counts(file_data, by_detector)
   279→    _apply_cohesion_complexity_counts(file_data, by_detector)
   280→    return _rank_complexity_entries(file_data)
   281→
   282→
   283→def _build_error_hotspots(by_detector: dict[str, list[dict]]) -> list[dict]:
   284→    """Files with 3+ exception handling findings from smells detector."""
   285→    error_smell_ids = frozenset({
   286→        "broad_except", "silent_except", "empty_except",
   287→        "swallowed_error", "bare_except",
   288→    })
   289→    file_counts: dict[str, dict[str, int]] = defaultdict(lambda: defaultdict(int))
   290→
   291→    for finding in by_detector.get("smells", []):
   292→        smell_id = _get_detail(finding, "smell_id", "")
   293→        if smell_id not in error_smell_ids:
   294→            continue
   295→        filepath = finding.get("file", "")
   296→        if filepath:
   297→            file_counts[filepath][smell_id] += 1
   298→
   299→    results = []
   300→    for filepath, counts in file_counts.items():
   301→        total = sum(counts.values())
   302→        if total < 3:
   303→            continue
   304→        entry = {"file": filepath, "total": total}
   305→        for sid in sorted(error_smell_ids):
   306→            entry[sid] = counts.get(sid, 0)
   307→        results.append(entry)
   308→    results.sort(key=lambda e: -e["total"])
   309→    return results[:20]
   310→
   311→
   312→def _build_mutable_globals(by_detector: dict[str, list[dict]]) -> list[dict]:
   313→    """All global_mutable_config findings."""
   314→    results: list[dict] = []
   315→    file_data: dict[str, dict] = {}
   316→
   317→    for finding in by_detector.get("global_mutable_config", []):
   318→        filepath = finding.get("file", "")
   319→        if not filepath:
   320→            continue
   321→        entry = file_data.setdefault(filepath, {
   322→            "file": filepath, "names": [], "total_mutations": 0,
   323→        })
   324→        name = _get_detail(finding, "name", "")
   325→        if name and name not in entry["names"]:
   326→            entry["names"].append(name)
   327→        mutations = _safe_num(_get_detail(finding, "mutations"))
   328→        entry["total_mutations"] += int(mutations) if mutations else 1
   329→
   330→    results = sorted(file_data.values(), key=lambda e: -e["total_mutations"])
   331→    return results[:20]
   332→
   333→
   334→def _build_boundary_violations(by_detector: dict[str, list[dict]]) -> list[dict]:
   335→    """From coupling and layer_violation detectors."""
   336→    results: list[dict] = []
   337→    for det_name in ("coupling", "layer_violation"):
   338→        for finding in by_detector.get(det_name, []):
   339→            filepath = finding.get("file", "")
   340→            detail = _detail_payload(finding)
   341→            results.append({
   342→                "file": filepath,
   343→                "target": detail["target"] or detail["imported_from"],
   344→                "direction": detail["direction"] or detail["violation"],
   345→            })
   346→    return results[:30]
   347→
   348→
   349→def _build_dead_code(by_detector: dict[str, list[dict]]) -> list[dict]:
   350→    """Orphaned files + uncalled functions."""
   351→    results: list[dict] = []
   352→    for finding in by_detector.get("orphaned", []):
   353→        filepath = finding.get("file", "")
   354→        signals = _get_signals(finding)
   355→        loc = _safe_num(signals.get("loc", _get_detail(finding, "loc")))
   356→        results.append({"file": filepath, "kind": "orphaned", "loc": int(loc)})
   357→    for finding in by_detector.get("uncalled_functions", []):
   358→        filepath = finding.get("file", "")
   359→        detail = finding.get("detail", {})
   360→        loc = _safe_num(detail.get("loc", 0)) if isinstance(detail, dict) else 0
   361→        results.append({"file": filepath, "kind": "uncalled", "loc": int(loc)})
   362→    return results[:30]
   363→
   364→
   365→def _build_private_crossings(by_detector: dict[str, list[dict]]) -> list[dict]:
   366→    """From private_imports detector."""
   367→    results: list[dict] = []
   368→    for finding in by_detector.get("private_imports", []):
   369→        filepath = finding.get("file", "")
   370→        detail = _detail_payload(finding)
   371→        results.append({
   372→            "file": filepath,
   373→            "symbol": detail["symbol"] or detail["name"],
   374→            "source": detail["source"] or detail["imported_from"],
   375→            "target": detail["target"] or filepath,
   376→        })
   377→    return results[:30]
   378→
   379→
   380→def _build_deferred_import_density(by_file: dict[str, list[dict]]) -> list[dict]:
   381→    """Files with 2+ deferred_import smells (proxy for cycle pressure)."""
   382→    file_counts: dict[str, int] = defaultdict(int)
   383→    for filepath, file_findings in by_file.items():
   384→        for finding in file_findings:
   385→            if finding.get("detector") != "smells":
   386→                continue
   387→            if _get_detail(finding, "smell_id") == "deferred_import":
   388→                file_counts[filepath] += 1
   389→
   390→    results = [
   391→        {"file": filepath, "count": count}
   392→        for filepath, count in file_counts.items()
   393→        if count >= 2
   394→    ]
   395→    results.sort(key=lambda e: -e["count"])
   396→    return results[:20]
   397→
   398→
   399→def _build_duplicate_clusters(by_detector: dict[str, list[dict]]) -> list[dict]:
   400→    """From dupes and boilerplate_duplication detectors."""
   401→    results: list[dict] = []
   402→    for det_name in ("dupes", "boilerplate_duplication"):
   403→        for finding in by_detector.get(det_name, []):
   404→            detail = _detail_payload(finding)
   405→            kind = detail["kind"] or det_name
   406→            name = detail["name"] or detail["function"] or finding.get("summary", "")[:60]
   407→            files = detail["files"]
   408→            if not isinstance(files, list):
   409→                files = [finding.get("file", "")]
   410→            results.append({
   411→                "kind": kind,
   412→                "cluster_size": len(files) if files else 1,
   413→                "name": name,
   414→                "files": files[:10],
   415→            })
   416→    return results[:20]
   417→
   418→
   419→def _build_naming_drift(by_detector: dict[str, list[dict]]) -> list[dict]:
   420→    """From naming detector."""
   421→    dir_data: dict[str, dict] = {}
   422→    for finding in by_detector.get("naming", []):
   423→        filepath = finding.get("file", "")
   424→        detail = _detail_payload(finding)
   425→        # Group by directory
   426→        parts = filepath.rsplit("/", 1)
   427→        directory = parts[0] + "/" if len(parts) > 1 else "./"
   428→        entry = dir_data.setdefault(directory, {
   429→            "directory": directory,
   430→            "majority": detail["expected_convention"],
   431→            "minority_count": 0,
   432→            "outliers": [],
   433→        })
   434→        entry["minority_count"] += 1
   435→        if filepath not in entry["outliers"]:
   436→            entry["outliers"].append(filepath)
   437→        if not entry["majority"] and detail["expected_convention"]:
   438→            entry["majority"] = detail["expected_convention"]
   439→
   440→    results = sorted(dir_data.values(), key=lambda e: -e["minority_count"])
   441→    return results[:20]
   442→
   443→
   444→def _build_flat_dir_findings(by_detector: dict[str, list[dict]]) -> list[dict]:
   445→    """From flat_dirs detector."""
   446→    results: list[dict] = []
   447→    for finding in by_detector.get("flat_dirs", []):
   448→        filepath = finding.get("file", "")
   449→        detail = _detail_payload(finding)
   450→        results.append({
   451→            "directory": filepath,
   452→            "kind": detail["kind"] or detail["reason"],
   453→            "file_count": int(_safe_num(detail["file_count"])),
   454→            "combined_score": int(
   455→                _safe_num(detail["score"] or detail["combined_score"])
   456→            ),
   457→        })
   458→    results.sort(key=lambda e: -e["combined_score"])
   459→    return results[:20]
   460→
   461→
   462→def _build_large_file_distribution(by_detector: dict[str, list[dict]]) -> dict | None:
   463→    """Distribution stats from structural findings."""
   464→    locs: list[float] = []
   465→    for finding in by_detector.get("structural", []):
   466→        signals = _get_signals(finding)
   467→        loc = _safe_num(signals.get("loc"))
   468→        if loc > 0:
   469→            locs.append(loc)
   470→    if not locs:
   471→        return None
   472→    locs.sort()
   473→    n = len(locs)
   474→    return {
   475→        "count": n,
   476→        "median_loc": int(locs[n // 2]),
   477→        "p90_loc": int(locs[int(n * 0.9)]) if n >= 10 else int(locs[-1]),
   478→        "p99_loc": int(locs[int(n * 0.99)]) if n >= 100 else int(locs[-1]),
   479→    }
   480→
   481→
   482→def _build_security_hotspots(by_detector: dict[str, list[dict]]) -> list[dict]:
   483→    """Files with 3+ security findings grouped by severity."""
   484→    file_counts: dict[str, dict[str, int]] = defaultdict(lambda: defaultdict(int))
   485→    for finding in by_detector.get("security", []):
   486→        filepath = finding.get("file", "")
   487→        if not filepath:
   488→            continue
   489→        detail = finding.get("detail", {})
   490→        severity = (
   491→            detail.get("severity", "medium")
   492→            if isinstance(detail, dict) else "medium"
   493→        )
   494→        file_counts[filepath][severity] += 1
   495→
   496→    results = []
   497→    for filepath, counts in file_counts.items():
   498→        total = sum(counts.values())
   499→        if total < 3:
   500→            continue
   501→        results.append({
   502→            "file": filepath,
   503→            "high_severity": counts.get("high", 0),
   504→            "medium_severity": counts.get("medium", 0),
   505→            "total": total,
   506→        })
   507→    results.sort(key=lambda e: (-e["high_severity"], -e["total"]))
   508→    return results[:20]
   509→
   510→
   511→def _build_signal_density(by_file: dict[str, list[dict]]) -> list[dict]:
   512→    """Top 20 files by number of distinct detectors firing."""
   513→    results: list[dict] = []
   514→    for filepath, file_findings in by_file.items():
   515→        detectors = set()
   516→        for finding in file_findings:
   517→            det = finding.get("detector", "")
   518→            if det:
   519→                detectors.add(det)
   520→        if len(detectors) >= 2:
   521→            results.append({
   522→                "file": filepath,
   523→                "detector_count": len(detectors),
   524→                "finding_count": len(file_findings),
   525→                "detectors": sorted(detectors),
   526→            })
   527→    results.sort(key=lambda e: (-e["detector_count"], -e["finding_count"]))
   528→    return results[:20]
   529→
   530→
   531→def _build_systemic_patterns(
   532→    smell_counter: Counter[str],
   533→    smell_files: dict[str, list[str]],
   534→) -> list[dict]:
   535→    """Smell subtypes appearing in 5+ files."""
   536→    results: list[dict] = []
   537→    for smell_id, _count in smell_counter.most_common():
   538→        unique_files = sorted(set(smell_files.get(smell_id, [])))
   539→        if len(unique_files) < 5:
   540→            continue
   541→        # Find top hotspot files (most occurrences)
   542→        file_counts = Counter(smell_files[smell_id])
   543→        hotspots = [
   544→            f"{f} ({c})" for f, c in file_counts.most_common(5)
   545→        ]
   546→        results.append({
   547→            "pattern": smell_id,
   548→            "file_count": len(unique_files),
   549→            "hotspots": hotspots,
   550→        })
   551→    return results[:20]
   552→
   553→
   554→__all__ = ["gather_mechanical_evidence"]
   555→
```

Now I have a thorough understanding of the full review pipeline. Here is my comprehensive report.

---

## Complete Report: How the LLM Reviewer is Prompted

### Overview of the Review Pipeline

The review system has two modes: **per-file review** and **holistic review** (the primary mode). The holistic review breaks the codebase into investigation batches, each targeting specific quality dimensions. A "blind packet" is prepared with all context but with score anchoring data stripped, so the LLM reviewer cannot anchor to existing scores.

---

### 1. System Prompt and Dimension Definitions

**Source file:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/languages/_framework/review_data/dimensions.json`

This is the single source of truth. It defines:

**20 default dimensions:**
`naming_quality`, `logic_clarity`, `type_safety`, `contract_coherence`, `error_consistency`, `abstraction_fitness`, `ai_generated_debt`, `high_level_elegance`, `mid_level_elegance`, `low_level_elegance`, `cross_module_architecture`, `initialization_coupling`, `convention_outlier`, `dependency_health`, `test_strategy`, `api_surface_coherence`, `authorization_consistency`, `incomplete_migration`, `package_organization`, `design_coherence`

**Each dimension prompt has three fields:**
- `description` -- what the dimension evaluates
- `look_for` -- specific patterns/signals the LLM should watch for
- `skip` -- things to NOT report (avoiding false positives)

**The system prompt** (lines 377 of dimensions.json) is a massive, highly structured instruction set given to the LLM. Key rules include:

1. **Defects only** -- findings must describe things that need to change, never positive observations
2. **Confidence calibration** -- high/medium/low with worked examples for each level
3. **Evidence-first scoring** -- start at 100 and subtract only for evidenced issues
4. **Global anchors** -- 100 = exemplary, 90 = strong, 80 = solid, 70 = mixed, 60 = significant drag, 40 = poor, 20 = severe
5. **Per-dimension anchors** -- every dimension has its own 100/90/80/60 rubric
6. **Import guard** -- scores below 100 must include explicit feedback; scores below 85 must include at least one finding
7. **Non-findings list** -- consistent patterns, tiny functions, framework boilerplate, style preferences

**Output format** required: JSON with `assessments` (dimension -> 0-100 score) and `findings` array (each with dimension, identifier, summary, related_files, evidence, suggestion, confidence).

**Feedback contract** (`/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/intelligence/review/feedback_contract.py`):
- `LOW_SCORE_FINDING_THRESHOLD = 85.0` -- scores below this MUST have findings
- `ASSESSMENT_FEEDBACK_THRESHOLD = 100.0` -- any non-100 score needs feedback
- `HIGH_SCORE_ISSUES_NOTE_THRESHOLD = 85.0` -- scores above this must include an `issues_preventing_higher_score` note
- `ensure_prompt_contract()` appends a "GLOBAL REVIEW CONTRACT" to every system prompt, reinforcing scope breadth, no-suppression, and structural thinking rules

---

### 2. Per-Language Overrides

**Source files:** `desloppify/languages/{lang}/review_data/dimensions.override.json`

Languages can customize prompts via an override mechanism in `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/intelligence/review/dimensions/data.py`. The `_apply_dimensions_override()` function supports:
- Replacing the default dimension list (`default_dimensions`)
- Appending/removing dimensions (`default_dimensions_append`/`_remove`)
- Overriding specific dimension prompts (`dimension_prompts`)
- Appending to the system prompt (`system_prompt_append`)

Example: TypeScript override (`/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/languages/typescript/review_data/dimensions.override.json`) replaces the `abstraction_fitness` prompt with TypeScript-specific look_for items (prop-drilling wrappers, single-implementation interfaces, generic surfaces) and appends a TS-specific system prompt suffix.

Language plugins can also provide `review_guidance` (via `get_lang_guidance()`) which gets included in the packet.

---

### 3. Context Building -- What the LLM Sees About the Codebase

Two levels of context are gathered:

#### A. Per-File ReviewContext (`/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/intelligence/review/context.py`)

Built by `build_review_context()`, which creates a `ReviewContext` dataclass with these sections:

| Section | What it contains |
|---------|-----------------|
| `naming_vocabulary` | Top 20 function/class name prefixes and total name count |
| `error_conventions` | Per-file error-handling pattern presence counts |
| `module_patterns` | Per-directory module pattern prevalence (class_based, etc.) |
| `import_graph_summary` | Top 20 most-imported files with importer counts |
| `zone_distribution` | File zone counts (production/test/config/generated/vendor) |
| `existing_findings` | Open findings per file (detector + summary, max 80 chars) |
| `codebase_stats` | total_files, total_loc, avg_file_loc |
| `sibling_conventions` | Per-directory function naming prefix distribution |
| `ai_debt_signals` | AI-generated code pattern detection per file |
| `auth_patterns` | [REDACTED] patterns |
| `error_strategies` | Dominant error strategy per file (classify_error_strategy) |

#### B. HolisticContext (`/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/intelligence/review/context_holistic/orchestrator.py`)

Built by `build_holistic_context()`, a much richer model with 14 sections:

| Section | What it contains |
|---------|-----------------|
| `architecture` | God modules, top-imported files |
| `coupling` | Module-level I/O, boundary violations |
| `conventions` | Naming by directory, sibling behavior analysis, duplicate clusters, naming drift |
| `errors` | Strategy by directory, exception hotspots, mutable globals |
| `abstractions` | Util files, pass-through wrappers, indirection hotspots, wide param bags, one-impl interfaces, delegation-heavy classes, facade modules, TypedDict violations, complexity hotspots |
| `dependencies` | Cycle files/summaries, deferred import density |
| `testing` | Critical untested paths (high-importer files missing test coverage) |
| `api_surface` | Sync/async mix, language-specific API surface analysis |
| `structure` | Directory profiles (file counts, fan-in/out), root files, coupling matrix |
| `codebase_stats` | File/LOC statistics |
| `authorization` | Route auth coverage, service role usage |
| `ai_debt_signals` | File-level AI debt signals |
| `migration_signals` | Deprecated markers, migration TODOs |
| `scan_evidence` | **Aggregated mechanical detector evidence** (see below) |

#### C. Mechanical Evidence (`/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/intelligence/review/context_holistic/mechanical.py`)

`gather_mechanical_evidence()` aggregates ALL open findings from state into structured clusters:

- `complexity_hotspots` -- top 20 files by composite complexity score (LOC, cyclomatic, nesting, params, monster functions)
- `error_hotspots` -- files with 3+ exception handling findings
- `mutable_globals` -- global mutable state findings
- `boundary_violations` -- coupling and layer violation findings
- `dead_code` -- orphaned files + uncalled functions
- `private_crossings` -- private import violations
- `deferred_import_density` -- files with 2+ deferred imports (cycle pressure proxy)
- `duplicate_clusters` -- cross-file function duplication
- `naming_drift` -- per-directory naming convention violations
- `flat_dir_findings` -- overloaded/fragmented directories
- `large_file_distribution` -- LOC distribution statistics
- `security_hotspots` -- files with 3+ security findings
- `signal_density` -- top 20 files by number of distinct detectors firing
- `systemic_patterns` -- smell subtypes appearing in 5+ files

---

### 4. Investigation Batches

**Source file:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/intelligence/review/prepare_batches.py`

`build_investigation_batches()` creates 9 independent batches from holistic context:

| Batch | Dimensions | Files from |
|-------|-----------|------------|
| Architecture & Coupling | cross_module_architecture, high_level_elegance | God modules, module-level I/O, boundary violations, deferred imports |
| Conventions & Errors | convention_outlier, error_consistency, mid_level_elegance | Sibling outliers, mixed error strategy dirs, exception hotspots, dupes, naming drift |
| Abstractions & Dependencies | abstraction_fitness, dependency_health, mid/low_level_elegance | Util files, wrappers, indirection, param bags, interfaces, delegation, facades, TypedDict violations, complexity, cycles |
| Testing & API | test_strategy, api_surface_coherence, mid_level_elegance | Critical untested paths, sync/async mix |
| Authorization | authorization_consistency, mid_level_elegance | Auth gaps, service role usage |
| AI Debt & Migrations | ai_generated_debt, incomplete_migration, low_level_elegance | AI debt signals, deprecated markers, migration TODOs |
| Package Organization | package_organization, high_level_elegance | Flat dir findings, root files, largest dirs, coupling matrix edges |
| State & Design Integrity | initialization_coupling, design_coherence | Mutable globals, complexity hotspots, error hotspots, signal density |
| Governance & Contracts | cross_module_architecture, high_level_elegance, test_strategy, package_organization | README, philosophy docs, pyproject.toml + top-imported anchor files |

A 10th "Design coherence -- Mechanical Concern Signals" batch is appended from `batch_concerns()` with concern hypothesis signals.

An optional "Full Codebase Sweep" batch covers all production files (disabled when dimensions are explicitly scoped).

Each batch contains: `name`, `dimensions`, `files_to_read`, `why` (rationale), and optionally `concern_signals` and `historical_issue_focus`.

---

### 5. Blind Packet Construction

**Source file:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/review/runtime/runner_helpers.py` (lines 735-748)

`_build_blind_packet()` strips score-anchoring information before the LLM sees the packet:

**Dropped keys:** `narrative`, `next_command`, `score_snapshot`, `strict_target`, `strict_target_progress`, `subjective_at_target`

**Sanitized config keys:** any key containing `target`, or ending in `_score`, or matching specific score hint keys (`target_strict_score`, `strict_score`, `overall_score`, `objective_score`, etc.)

This is a core anti-gaming measure -- the reviewer cannot see current scores or targets.

---

### 6. Per-Batch Subagent Prompt

**Source file:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/review/payload/batch_prompt_template.py`

`render_batch_prompt()` constructs the actual prompt each subagent runner gets. Structure:

1. **Metadata block**: "You are a focused subagent reviewer for a single holistic investigation batch." + repo root, blind packet path, batch index/name/dimensions/rationale

2. **Scan evidence note**: Points to `holistic_context.scan_evidence` for investigative leads

3. **Seed files block**: Listed files to start review from

4. **Historical issue focus** (if retrospective mode): "Previously flagged issues" with status/summary/notes, with instructions to check if they still exist

5. **Mechanical concern signals** (if present): Detector synthesis hypotheses with type/file/summary/question/evidence, labeled as hypotheses to confirm or refute

6. **Task requirements** (13 numbered rules):
   - Follow blind packet `system_prompt` constraints
   - Use previously flagged issues as context (don't re-report fixed/wontfix)
   - Confirm or refute mechanical concern signals
   - Think structurally about root causes
   - Explore beyond seed files (prioritize hotspot neighbors)
   - Keep scoped to batch dimensions
   - 0-N findings cap (N = max of 10 or dimension count)
   - Score/finding consistency required
   - Scores below 85.0 MUST include finding
   - Every finding needs related_files, dimension, identifier, summary, evidence, suggestion, confidence, impact_scope, fix_scope
   - Every scored dimension MUST include dimension_notes with evidence
   - Scores above 85.0 MUST include `issues_preventing_higher_score`

7. **Dimension-specific focus** (conditional):
   - `package_organization`: ground in objective structure signals, thresholded evidence
   - `abstraction_fitness`: use delegation_heavy_classes, facade_modules, typed_dict_violations, complexity_hotspots
   - `initialization_coupling`: use mutable_globals evidence
   - `design_coherence`: use signal_density, complexity_hotspots
   - `error_consistency`: use exception_hotspots
   - `cross_module_architecture`: use boundary_violations, deferred_import_density
   - `convention_outlier`: use duplicate_clusters, naming_drift
   - Architecture/integration dimensions: workflow integrity checks (loop-prone patterns, blind spots)

8. **Scope enums**: impact_scope and fix_scope value definitions

9. **Output schema**: exact JSON structure expected (batch, batch_index, assessments, dimension_notes with evidence/impact_scope/fix_scope/confidence/sub_axes, findings with full schema, retrospective with root_causes/likely_symptoms/possible_false_positives)

---

### 7. Issue History / Retrospective Context

**Source file:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/intelligence/review/_prepare/issue_history.py`

When `--retrospective` flag is used:

`build_issue_history_context()` gathers all review-detector findings from state and produces:
- **Summary**: total review findings, open count, status counts, dimension open counts
- **Recent issues** (most recent N by `last_seen`): each shaped as `{dimension, status, summary, suggestion, related_files, note, confidence, first_seen, last_seen}`

Auto-resolve notes ("not reported in latest holistic re-import") are filtered out as non-meaningful.

`build_batch_issue_focus()` then slices the history to only matching dimensions for each batch, capped at `max_items` (default 20).

The batch prompt template renders these as:
```
Previously flagged issues (from past reviews of these dimensions):
Check whether each issue still exists in the current code. Do not re-report
issues that have been fixed or marked wontfix -- focus on what remains or what is new.
  - [open] Summary text (note: human note if any)
  - [fixed] Summary text
```

---

### 8. Remediation Plan Generation

**Source file:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/intelligence/review/_prepare/remediation_engine.py`

`generate_remediation_plan()` is NOT an LLM-prompted step -- it is a **deterministic** markdown generator that:

1. Collects all open holistic findings (detector=review, holistic=true)
2. Weights them by confidence using `CONFIDENCE_WEIGHTS * HOLISTIC_MULTIPLIER`
3. Sorts by weight (highest impact first)
4. Renders a markdown plan with: header (current scores), usage instructions, and per-finding entries with priority number, dimension, confidence, impact estimate, evidence, suggested fix, related files, reasoning, and a resolve command

The plan includes estimated point impact (`impact_pts`) based on `weight / potential * 100`.

---

### 9. Scoring Pipeline

**Source file:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_scoring/subjective/core.py`

`append_subjective_dimensions()` takes imported review assessments and integrates them into the scoring system:

- Assessments with `reset_by=scan_reset_subjective` or `placeholder=True` are treated as zeroed out (pending fresh review)
- `integrity_penalty=target_match_reset` also zeros the score
- Each dimension gets: `score`, `pass_rate` (score/100), `weighted_failures`, `assessment_score` (original imported score), `configured_weight`, `components`, and `component_scores`

**Batch scoring** (`/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/review/payload/batch_scoring.py`):

`DimensionMergeScorer` merges multi-batch results:
- Weighted mean of scores across batches (weighted by confidence)
- Floor blend: 70% weighted mean + 30% lowest per-batch score
- Issue penalty: capped at 24 points, scaled by finding severity (confidence * impact_scope * fix_scope)
- Issue cap: findings cap the max score (ceiling 90, floor 60, proportional to severity)

---

### 10. Complete Data Flow Summary

```
CLI: desloppify review --prepare --path <src>
  |
  v
packet_builder.build_review_packet()
  |-- prepare_holistic_review()
  |     |-- build_holistic_context() --> HolisticContext (14 sections)
  |     |-- build_review_context() --> ReviewContext (11 sections)
  |     |-- load_dimensions_for_lang() --> dims, prompts, system_prompt
  |     |-- build_investigation_batches() --> 9 themed batches
  |     |-- batch_concerns() --> mechanical concern batch
  |     |-- build_issue_history_context() --> retrospective data (if --retrospective)
  |     |-- filter_batches_to_dimensions() --> scoped batches
  |     |-- filter_batches_to_file_scope() --> production-only files
  |     v
  |   Full holistic review packet
  |
  |-- Add narrative, config (redacted), next_command
  v
query.json (written to .desloppify/query.json)
  |
  v
Runner: desloppify review --run-batches --runner codex --parallel
  |
  |-- write_packet_snapshot() --> immutable + blind packet
  |     |-- _build_blind_packet() strips score hints
  |
  |-- For each batch:
  |     render_batch_prompt() builds subagent prompt
  |       |-- metadata block (repo root, blind packet path, batch info)
  |       |-- scan evidence note
  |       |-- seed files
  |       |-- historical issue focus (retrospective)
  |       |-- mechanical concern signals
  |       |-- 13 task requirement rules
  |       |-- dimension-specific focus instructions
  |       |-- scope enums
  |       |-- output schema
  |     v
  |   Subagent runs, reads blind packet + code, returns JSON
  |
  |-- merge_batch_results() combines multi-batch outputs
  |     |-- DimensionMergeScorer for pressure-adjusted scores
  |
  |-- do_import() writes findings + assessments to state
  v
State updated with review findings and assessment scores
```

The LLM reviewer ultimately sees:
1. The **blind packet** (full holistic context, review context, dimension prompts, system prompt -- but NO scores/targets/narrative)
2. The **batch-specific prompt** (seed files, historical issues, mechanical concern hypotheses, rules, output schema)
3. The actual **repository code** (read from disk during review)

```json
{
  "command": "python3 -c \"\nimport json\nwith open('.desloppify/state-python.json') as f:\n    state = json.load(f)\n\n# Get ALL review findings - print id, status, summary, dimension, first_seen, last_seen\nfindings = state.get('findings', {})\nreview_findings = []\nfor fid, f in findings.items():\n    if f.get('detector') == 'review' or f.get('dimension') == 'review':\n        review_findings.append({\n            'id': fid,\n            'status': f.get('status'),\n            'dimension': f.get('dimension'),\n            'summary': f.get('summary', '')[:200],\n            'first_seen': f.get('first_seen'),\n            'last_seen': f.get('last_seen'),\n            'resolved_at': f.get('resolved_at'),\n            'tier': f.get('tier'),\n            'file': f.get('file', ''),\n        })\n\nprint(f'Total review findings: {len(review_findings)}')\nprint()\n\n# Sort by first_seen\nreview_findings.sort(key=lambda x: x.get('first_seen') or '')\nfor rf in review_findings:\n    print(f'[{rf[\\\"status\\\"]:12s}] {rf[\\\"first_seen\\\"]} -> {rf[\\\"last_seen\\\"]}')\n    print(f'  File: {rf[\\\"file\\\"]}')\n    print(f'  Summary: {rf[\\\"summary\\\"]}')\n    print(f'  Resolved: {rf[\\\"resolved_at\\\"]}')\n    print()\n\" 2>&1 | head -300\n",
  "description": "Get all review findings with timestamps"
}
```

```json
{
  "command": "python3 -c \"\nimport json\nwith open('.desloppify/state-python.json') as f:\n    state = json.load(f)\n\n# Find ALL review findings - both open and resolved - sorted by first_seen\nfindings = state.get('findings', {})\nreview_all = []\nfor fid, f in findings.items():\n    det = f.get('detector', '')\n    dim = f.get('dimension', '')\n    if det == 'review' or dim == 'review':\n        review_all.append((fid, f))\n\n# Also look for any finding with 'package_organization' in the id or summary\npkg_org = []\nfor fid, f in findings.items():\n    if 'package_organization' in fid or 'package_organization' in f.get('summary', '').lower() or 'package_organization' in f.get('dimension', ''):\n        pkg_org.append((fid, f))\n\nprint(f'Total review findings (all statuses): {len(review_all)}')\nprint(f'Findings mentioning package_organization: {len(pkg_org)}')\nprint()\n\n# Count review findings by status\nfrom collections import Counter\nstatus_counts = Counter(f.get('status') for _, f in review_all)\nprint('Review findings by status:')\nfor s, c in status_counts.most_common():\n    print(f'  {s}: {c}')\nprint()\n\n# Show the resolved/fixed review findings\nprint('=== Resolved/Fixed Review Findings ===')\nresolved_reviews = [(fid, f) for fid, f in review_all if f.get('status') in ('fixed', 'auto_resolved', 'wontfix', 'resolved')]\nresolved_reviews.sort(key=lambda x: x[1].get('first_seen', ''))\nfor fid, f in resolved_reviews:\n    print(f'[{f[\\\"status\\\"]:12s}] first_seen: {f.get(\\\"first_seen\\\")}  resolved_at: {f.get(\\\"resolved_at\\\")}')\n    print(f'  ID: {fid}')\n    print(f'  Summary: {f.get(\\\"summary\\\", \\\"\\\")[:200]}')\n    print()\n\"\n",
  "description": "Get all review findings by status, including resolved"
}
```

```json
{
  "command": "python3 -c \"\nimport json\n# Read all the canonical import files chronologically\nimport glob, os\n\nfiles = sorted(glob.glob('.desloppify/external_review_sessions/*/canonical_import_*.json'))\nfor f in files:\n    with open(f) as fh:\n        data = json.load(fh)\n    assessments = data.get('assessments', {})\n    findings = data.get('findings', [])\n    po_score = assessments.get('package_organization', 'N/A')\n    provenance = data.get('provenance', {})\n    ts = provenance.get('created_at', os.path.basename(f))\n    \n    # Count findings by dimension\n    from collections import Counter\n    dim_counts = Counter()\n    for finding in findings:\n        d = finding.get('dimension', 'unknown')\n        dim_counts[d] += 1\n    \n    print(f'=== {ts} (session: {provenance.get(\\\"external_session_id\\\", \\\"?\\\")}) ===')\n    print(f'  package_organization score: {po_score}')\n    print(f'  Total findings: {len(findings)}')\n    if dim_counts:\n        print(f'  Findings by dimension: {dict(dim_counts)}')\n    # Print package_organization related findings\n    for finding in findings:\n        if finding.get('dimension') == 'package_organization':\n            print(f'  [PO finding] {finding.get(\\\"summary\\\", \\\"\\\")[:150]}')\n    print()\n\"\n",
  "description": "Read ALL canonical import files chronologically to trace assessment history"
}
```

Now I have the complete picture. Here is the full timeline and analysis.

---

## Package Organization Dimension: Complete Timeline

### Background: How the Dimension Works

The "Structure nav" dimension (internal key: `package_organization`) is a **subjective assessment** scored by an external AI reviewer. It is not measured by any mechanical detector -- it is scored holistically by examining the codebase's directory structure, module boundaries, and import paths. The score feeds into the overall "strict score" with a configured weight of 5.0.

---

### Phase 1: Early Reviews -- Perfect Score, No Signal (Feb 18-23)

**Feb 18**: The major architectural overhaul commit (`d85a3b0`) shipped. This was the "catalytic overhaul" that reorganized the codebase from a flatter structure into the current layered architecture: `app/commands/`, `core/`, `engine/`, `intelligence/`, `languages/`. It created the `review/` package with 17 files at that level.

**Feb 22**: A rapid succession of cleanup commits followed:
- `3dd2d35` -- "Major architecture cleanup: migrate imports to canonical sources, flatten modules"
- `3ee97ba` -- "Deep architecture cleanup: remove utils re-exports, create work_queue facade"
- `345c440` -- "Resolve all 18 review findings: structural refactors, type safety, test coverage"

**Feb 23, 20:06** -- First external review session (`ext_20260223_200643_020151cc`):
- **package_organization: 100** (perfect)
- **0 findings**
- This was a flawed review -- ALL 12 dimensions scored 100, with zero findings. The reviewer essentially rubber-stamped everything.

**Feb 23, 20:10-20:15** -- Several more review attempts, some failed (bad JSON), some gave 100s across the board with 0 findings.

**Feb 23, 21:45** -- Holistic review session (`ext_20260223_214555_46a6eacd`):
- Only assessed `duplication: 100`
- Not a meaningful review for structural dimensions.

At this point, the system had no real signal on package_organization. The perfect 100 scores were likely from poor reviewer calibration.

---

### Phase 2: First Real Findings (Feb 28, ~01:15)

**Between Feb 23-28**, significant work happened: living plan engine shipped, resolve command consolidated, runner_helpers decomposed, review workflow improvements, etc.

**Feb 28, 01:15** -- First substantive review (`ext_20260228_011504_99ffccd6`):
- **package_organization: 96.1** (down from 100)
- **2 package_organization findings** out of 31 total findings
- **31 total findings** across many dimensions -- this was the first real review

The two findings flagged:

1. **"Review command package is a flat, overloaded directory with mixed concerns"** -- The `app/commands/review/` directory had 17 files / 6,848 LOC with mixed responsibilities. The reviewer specifically suggested creating `review/orchestration/`, `review/importing/`, and `review/context/` subdirectories.

2. **"Core package has competing facade vs concrete import paths"** -- `core/discovery_api.py`, `core/output_api.py`, and `core/file_paths.py` created an ambiguous boundary. The reviewer suggested creating `core/api/discovery.py`, `core/api/output.py`, `core/api/paths.py` as canonical surfaces.

**Feb 28, 01:54** -- Another review attempt (`ext_20260228_015353_8ef679b1`): 
- **package_organization: 100**, 0 findings
- Again a rubber-stamp review, not useful.

---

### Phase 3: The Response -- Structural Reorganization (Feb 28, afternoon-evening)

In response to the Feb 28 01:15 review findings, an uncommitted restructuring was performed (all changes visible in `git status` but not yet committed). Here is exactly what was done:

**For the `review/` flat directory problem**, the following subpackages were created:
- `review/orchestration/` -- `helpers.py` (11 LOC) + `runtime_defaults.py` (7 LOC) = 19 LOC total
- `review/quality/` -- `assessment_integrity.py` (57 LOC, moved from flat `review/assessment_integrity.py`)
- `review/importing/` -- `import_helpers.py` (44KB, moved from flat `review/import_helpers.py`)
- `review/normalization/` -- `batch_core.py` (887 LOC, moved from flat `review/batch_core.py`)
- `review/payload/` -- `batch_prompt_template.py` + `batch_scoring.py` + `constants.py` + `packet_builder.py`
- `review/runtime/` -- `runner_helpers.py` (54KB!) + `policy.py` + `setup.py` + `__init__.py` (with exports)

The deleted flat files:
- `assessment_integrity.py` (57 lines) -> `quality/assessment_integrity.py`
- `batch_core.py` (757 lines) -> `normalization/batch_core.py`
- `batch_prompt_template.py` (402 lines) -> `payload/batch_prompt_template.py`
- `batch_scoring.py` (176 lines) -> `payload/batch_scoring.py`
- `helpers.py` (11 lines) -> `orchestration/helpers.py`
- `import_helpers.py` (981 lines) -> `importing/import_helpers.py`
- `runner_helpers.py` (1487 lines) -> `runtime/runner_helpers.py`
- `runtime.py` (69 lines) -> `runtime/setup.py` (or similar)

**For the `core/` API boundary problem**, three new subpackages were created:
- `core/discovery/` -- `__init__.py`, `api.py` (88 lines, forwarding to `core/source_discovery`), `impl.py` (star-import shim)
- `core/io/` -- `__init__.py`, `api.py` (64 lines, forwarding to `core/output`), `impl.py` (star-import shim)
- `core/pathing/` -- `__init__.py`, `api.py` (109 lines, forwarding to `core/file_paths`), `impl.py` (star-import shim)

Additionally, a `compat/` shim layer was created:
- `compat/__init__.py`
- `compat/file_discovery.py` (re-exporting from `core.source_discovery`)
- `compat/scoring.py` (re-exporting from engine scoring)
- `compat/utils.py` (re-exporting from original utils, with its own `compute_tool_hash()`)

---

### Phase 4: The Review Backlash (Feb 28, ~23:53)

**Feb 28, 23:53** -- New external review (`ext_20260228_233656_9ea6b4e8`):
- **package_organization: 53.5** -- **crashed from 96.1 to 53.5**, a 42.6 point drop
- **6 package_organization findings** (up from 2)
- **68 total findings** (up from 31)

The reviewer flagged the exact restructuring that was done to fix the previous findings:

1. **"core/discovery/, core/io/, core/pathing/ are pure pass-through wrappers, creating 322 LOC of forwarding code"** -- The reviewer saw the new API packages as pointless shim layers that just forward every call 1-to-1 to the underlying modules.

2. **"review/orchestration/ is a 3-file package with 19 LOC total"** -- The package was too thin to justify a directory. One 11-line function and one constant.

3. **"review/quality/ contains exactly one 77-line file"** -- Single-file package with one consumer. Should be a flat file.

4. **"review/importing/ wraps exactly one large file (1301 LOC)"** -- A single file in a sub-package with empty `__init__.py`.

5. **"review/normalization/ wraps exactly one file (887 LOC)"** -- Same pattern.

6. **"core/ has 24 files alongside 3 subpackages that duplicate the same functionality through shim layers"** -- The restructuring created parallel structure: both `core/output.py` and `core/io/api.py` exist for the same concern.

**Related findings also emerged across other dimensions:**
- **Abstraction fit (42.0, down from 49.0)**: 8 findings about the pure pass-through facades -- `core/io/api.py`, `core/discovery/api.py`, `core/pathing/api.py`, `state_access.py`, `coercions_api.py`, all flagged as "zero value-add indirection"
- **Incomplete migration (42.0)**: `impl.py` files have zero importers, scoring chain is 3 hops, review legacy aliases still active
- **AI generated debt (38.0)**: `state_access.py` and `io/api.py` flagged as pure forwarding -- "generated code that adds maintenance burden without value"
- **Cross-module arch (62.0, up from 50.8)**: Engine importing from app layer, facade indirection explosion, dead compat shims

---

### Score Evolution Summary

| Date | Event | PO Score | Overall Score |
|------|-------|----------|---------------|
| Feb 23 20:06 | First review (rubber stamp) | 100 | N/A |
| Feb 28 01:15 | First real review, 2 findings | 96.1 | N/A |
| Feb 28 ~16:00 | Structural reorganization (uncommitted) | -- | -- |
| Feb 28 22:56 | Scan after restructuring | 49.3 | 76.8 |
| Feb 28 23:53 | New review, 6 findings | 53.5 | 76.1 |

---

### The Ironic Pattern

The timeline reveals a textbook "refactoring whack-a-mole" cycle:

1. **Round 1 reviewer said**: "review/ is too flat, create subdirectories" and "core/ has ambiguous API surfaces, create canonical subpackages."

2. **Response**: Created 6 new subdirectories under `review/` and 3 under `core/`, plus a `compat/` layer. Files were moved from flat locations into these subdirectories.

3. **Round 2 reviewer said**: "Those new subdirectories are over-structured -- you created packages for single files, pure pass-through wrappers, and 19-LOC packages. And now core/ has BOTH flat files AND subpackages for the same concern."

The restructuring followed the first reviewer's suggestion almost literally (the suggested subdirectory names match exactly), but the execution created the inverse problem: **premature decomposition** rather than the original **flat sprawl**. The reviewer wanted the files split across meaningful boundaries, but the actual split put single files into their own packages without enough content to justify the package overhead.

The current plan.json has auto-generated a cluster `auto/review-package_organization` with 6 findings, all Tier 1 priority, suggesting: "Merge into parent review/ directory" and "Make structured packages canonical and remove redundant flat files."
